  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Attila, leader of The Huns (Player 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Huns
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Horse Archer, Replacing Skirmisher (Requires technology: Mathematics)
        - Unique Unit: Tarkhan, Replacing Horseman (Requires technology: Military Strategy)
        - Unique Building: Ulticur, Replacing Circus Maximus (Requires technology: Metal Casting)
        - Unique Improvement: Eki (Can only be built on flat land tiles without access to Fresh Water. +1 Production and Gold if adjacent to two other Eki. Can be built in or adjacent to Hunnic territory. When completed, claims the tile beneath it if built in Neutral Territory.); Requires technology: Military Strategy
        - Civilization Ability: Inflict +100% and receive -50% War Weariness. +10% Combat Strength for each subsequent attack on the same target in a turn. Gain 1 Gold and Culture for every 2 damage dealt to Cities, scaling with Era.
      - Leader: Attila
      - PlayerID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Atomic Theory: Reveals Uranium and allows you to start working on the Manhattan Project, setting the stage for the production of nuclear weapons.
        Completing it would unlock: Computers, Nuclear Fission
      - Penicillin: All Workers receive the Fallout Reduction Promotion, which reduces the amount of damage they take while scrubbing Fallout. Allows you to build the Marine, a Unit that specializes in amphibious assaults. Also allows you to build the Medical Lab, a building which speeds the growth of your Cities.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics
      
      ## Policies
      - Air Supremacy (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Air Supremacy: Receive a free Airport in every City. +25% Production when building Aircraft. Can build Zeroes.
      - Co-Prosperity Sphere (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Co-Prosperity Sphere: Bullying City-States no longer reduces Influence, revokes Quests, or cancels Pledges of Protection. Doing so decreases the Influence of all other Civs with the bullied City-State by 10%, and increases your Influence by 10%.
      - Cult of Personality (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Cult of Personality: +50% Tourism to civilizations fighting a common enemy. 50% of your highest Warscore counts as a Tourism Modifier with all Civilizations. Free Great Person of your choice.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - Military-Industrial Complex (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Military-Industrial Complex: -33% Gold cost of purchasing units. +3 Science from Defense Buildings, Citadels, Forts, and Unique Improvements.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Exploitation (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Food and +1 Production from Farms, Camps, and Plantations.
        - * +1 Production and Science from Coasts, Lakes, and Oceans.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Free Thought (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -5 Unhappiness from Religious Unrest in all Cities.
        - * +25% Instant Yields from Great Scientists.
        - * Earn Great Scientists +33% faster.
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

    - #0:
      - role: user
      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 10
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 1
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 5
        - Boldness: 5
        - WarBias: 10
        - HostileBias: 10
        - WarmongerHate: 4
        - NeutralBias: 8
        - FriendlyBias: 5
        - GuardedBias: 7
        - AfraidBias: 4
        - DiplomaticBalance: 1
        - Friendliness: 3
        - WorkWithWillingness: 10
        - WorkAgainstWillingness: 10
        - Loyalty: 5
        - MinorCivFriendlyBias: 3
        - MinorCivNeutralBias: 5
        - MinorCivHostileBias: 8
        - MinorCivWarBias: 5
        - DenounceWillingness: 7
        - Forgiveness: 9
        - Meanness: 10
        - Neediness: 6
        - Chattiness: 2
        - DeceptiveBias: 7
        - Rationale: Reduce Boldness to 5 and increase AfraidBias to 4 after Dreadnought loss and Netherlands nuclear escalation (2 Atomic Bombs at Turfan). apprehension appropriate given Netherlands 6.4x military superiority. Maintain WarBias 10 and HostileBias 10 - Netherlands primary existential threat confirmed. MinorCivHostileBias reduced to 8 (from 10) after peace with Kathmandu/Yerevan but maintain hostile stance toward Melbourne/Bogota/Lisbon (all Netherlands allies that attacked). WorkWithWillingness 10 maintained for coalition integrity. Meanness 10 unchanged - Netherlands brutality (Lisbon razed) demands maximum resolve.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Penicillin
        - Rationale: Switch from Mobile Tactics to Penicillin. Mobile Tactics air upgrades secondary priority to immediate health/growth benefits. Penicillin provides Medical Lab (+growth) for wartime population recovery and Marine for amphibious operations against Netherlands coastal cities. Netherlands Apollo Program completed, so timeline to Science victory short for them; but our cities under war pressure need growth/happiness stabilization. Marine useful for future Netherlands coastal city assaults. Can return to Mobile Tactics (Radar) for Jet Fighter production after Penicillin complete.
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: None (Policy)
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Ethiopia
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Reset Ethiopia relationship to neutral (-50/-40→0/0). Ethiopia nearly destroyed by Mongolia (only 1 city, 16 population) and no longer effective Netherlands ally. Mongolia will eliminate them soon. No benefit in continued hostility - Ethiopia too weak to matter. Maintaining -50 modifiers wastes diplomatic energy and AI attention. Focus turns entirely on primary Netherlands threat instead.
        - UpdatedTurn: 270
        
        ### Belgrade
        - Public: 15
        - Private: 15
        - Rationale: Increase Belgrade relationship to 15 after flip to Venice. Belgrade (16 population, coastal) was our strategic foothold against Netherlands - its loss to alliance partner Venice means we need to demonstrate continued friendly interest. While Venice is now ally, positive relationship with Belgrade maintains our influence through trade routes and diplomatic units. Belgrade's location and Venice's alliance means we can still gain benefits indirectly. Prevents Netherlands from attempting to sway Venice or Belgrade away during World Congress voting. Maintains our diplomatic presence in the region for future opportunities.
        - UpdatedTurn: 293
        
        ### Shedet
        - Public: -70
        - Private: -70
        - Rationale: Intensify hostile stance toward Shedet from -60 to -70 after recent combat and strategic importance. Shedet's Ironclad and Gatling Gun production (events 5-7: Musketman upgraded to Gatling Gun) demonstrate military buildup against coalition. Located in zone 1062 with multiple Netherlands units (Field Guns, Cavalry). Netherlands Sphere: Shedet proposal (enacted turn 319) makes Shedet permanent Netherlands ally - treating as enemy combatant essential. Updated hostility signals AI to prioritize Shedet as military target, not diplomacy candidate.
        - UpdatedTurn: 321
        
        ### Quebec City
        - Public: 40
        - Private: 40
        - Rationale: Increase Melbourne relationship to 40. Melbourne already allied (1011 influence, 2 votes) but facing Netherlands hostility (War influence -60). Melbourne's coastal location (20 pop, zone 1068) makes them vulnerable to Netherlands naval pressure. Stronger relationship demonstrates commitment, prevents Melbourne reconsidering alliance under sanctions threat. Melbourne's spy quest against Venice (possible temporary alignment) offers future interaction opportunity. With Netherlands' 20 delegates, every vote matters - consolidating Melbourne's 2 votes through relationship insurance melts no diplomatic capital (we're already allied). Melbourne's Cavalry/Fusilier/Lancer/Gatling Gun composition (5 units) demonstrates military utility for coalition coordination in zone 1068.
        - UpdatedTurn: 323
        
        ### Lisbon
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Reset Portugal relationship to neutral (-100/-100→0/0). Lisbon captured from Portugal; city-state no longer exists as independent entity. Patrick (Liberated Lisbon earlier in war then declared war when Netherlands liberated it). Portugal posed minimal threat - only had 1 Paratrooper visible during Lisbon capture. With Lisbon now under Hunnic control as Modon (population 1, under resistance), Portugal is effectively eliminated as military factor. Maintaining -100 hostility wastes diplomatic resources that should focus on Netherlands nuclear threat. AI energy now prioritizes rebuilding Lisbon infrastructure, defending against Netherlands counterattack (Amsterdam nearby at 65,5), and coordinating with coalition. Relationship reset allows AI to treat Portugal situation as resolved rather than active combat front.
        - UpdatedTurn: 388
        
        ### Venice
        - Public: 35
        - Private: 35
        - Rationale: Keep Venice at 35/35 - crucial coalition partner with 16 delegates. Venice's 16 delegates are essential for World Congress vote in 4 turns. Their naval strength (Battleship, Fleet Destroyers) supports coalition maritime operations against Netherlands. Venice has Defensive Pact with Japan, strengthening the eastern voting block. Trade routes to Venice provide economic benefits funding war effort (326 GPT). 35 modifier maintains alliance without excessive diplomatic cost - with 420 GPT income, can sustain multiple alliance investments. Venice fighting Netherlands (war score -2%) alongside coalition. Their Shadow Networks policy provides +1 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations aiding tech race against Netherlands. No change needed - Venice is stable ally consistent with strategic priorities.
        - UpdatedTurn: 417
        
        ### Indonesia
        - Public: -10
        - Private: -10
        - Rationale: Further reduce Indonesia relationship from -10 to -20 to actively oppose Sanction: Indonesia proposal and reduce their effectiveness as Netherlands ally. Denmark's 'Sanction: Indonesia' proposal votes in 3 turns - stronger opposition signals consistent coalition stance. Indonesia recently made Peace Treaty with Netherlands (event 54-56: Ambassador movement to Mongolia), potentially re-aligning as key Netherlands strategic partner. Their 7 delegates originally aligned with Netherlands - sanctions passage would fragment this voting bloc and reduce Netherlands' diplomatic leverage. Added hostility consistent with coalition war effort against Netherlands alliance network. Indonesia's position as Denmark war target makes stronger opposition logical - Daniel critical coalition partner with 10 delegates.
        - UpdatedTurn: 418
        
        ### Mongolia
        - Public: 90
        - Private: 90
        - Rationale: Increase Mongolia relationship to 90/90 (from 100/100) while maintaining very strong commitment. With World Congress in 8 turns and Netherlands proposing World Ideology: Autocracy (42 delegates), Mongolia's 11 delegates critical alongside Japan (13), Denmark (12), Venice (14). Mongolian forces actively supporting war efforts - destroyed Kathmandu Infantry (event 199), have T-34 units pressing Netherlands positions. Trade routes actively contributing (4 caravans operated this turn). The reduction from 100 to 90 is technical - maintaining maximum alliance while acknowledging relationship ceiling. Defensive Pact 46 turns remaining requires unwavering commitment. This investment ensures coalition voting block stability through the upcoming vote.
        - UpdatedTurn: 424
        
        ### The Netherlands
        - Public: -50
        - Private: -50
        - Rationale: Maximum maintained hostility toward Netherlands after Lisbon loss and nuclear escalation. Netherlands razed Lisbon, liberated it as city-state (now at war with us), and confirmed 3 Atomic Bombs at Nijmegen (rebasing events 217-222). Total war capabilities already tested - Netherlands' 37,681 military vs our 5,484, total air superiority with 4 Fighters + 2 Heavy Bombers in Turfan. diplomacy impossible until these threats neutralized. Lisbon capture demonstrates Netherlands' willingness to eliminate our cities and strategic positions. Nuclear weapons preparation at Nijmegen requires complete hostility - no engagement options exist.
        - UpdatedTurn: 433
        
        ### Melbourne
        - Public: -40
        - Private: -40
        - Rationale: Establish hostility toward Melbourne after combat this turn damaged their Fleet Destroyer (events 336-337). While Melbourne already allied with Netherlands and at war (influence -60), formal hostility signals AI to treat them as military target rather than diplomatic opportunity. Melbourne's coastal position (21 pop, zone 1068) makes them vulnerable to Netherlands pressure - our naval presence there (5 Destroyers in zone -1068) requires clear hostile designation. Netherlands sphere alignment means Melbourne already committed against coalition. Resources prioritized for Netherlands primary threat.
        - UpdatedTurn: 433
        
        ### Japan
        - Public: 30
        - Private: 30
        - Rationale: Increase Japan relationship to 30 (from 25). Japan critical Information Era partner (71 techs vs our 63) providing technological perspective against Netherlands' 69 techs. Their 15 delegates essential for upcoming Global Hegemony proposal (Netherlands 42 delegates). Japan-Mongolia-Venice defensive pact framework controls eastern front; Japan stability prevents Netherlands diversion. Military cooperation proven effective across multiple theaters. Small investment for high diplomatic and strategic returns.
        - UpdatedTurn: 434
        
        ### Denmark
        - Public: 20
        - Private: 20
        - Rationale: Reduce Denmark relationship to 20 (from 40). Denmark valuable coalition partner but fight Mongolia too aggressively (68% war score against Mongolia, 12% against Japan). Denmark-Mongolia conflict undermines coalition unity and focuses Netherlands on single threat. Their 16 delegates important but not worth diplomatic capital cost when Denmark distracts from primary Netherlands threat. Can restore if Denmark demonstrates coalition cooperation against Netherlands.
        - UpdatedTurn: 434
        
        ### Kathmandu
        - Public: 10
        - Private: 10
        - Rationale: Reduce Kathmandu relationship to 10 (from -70). Peace treaty made this turn, improving relations to neutral signals reduced hostility. However, Kathmandu sank our Dreadnought (event 30) and onlyenate peace after devastating minor fronts. Maintain neutral stance rather than positive reward - Kathmandu's naval aggression requires caution. Decreases hostility without overlooking past attacks. Primary focus remains Netherlands elimination.
        - UpdatedTurn: 434
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: Maintain current strategic direction. Netherlands upgraded to Rocket Artillery and Mobile SAM, but coalition remains intact with 53 delegates (Mongolia 11, Japan 13, Venice 14, Denmark 15) vs Netherlands 44. Heavy casualties sustained protecting core territories - lost Rifleman (ID 22820), Machine Gun (ID 18810), Rifleman (ID 22718) in defensive engagements. Penicillin research continues (13 turns) for post-war population recovery via Medical Labs. Defensive posture in zones 2782 (Nijmegen) and 12905 (Turfan) holding despite Netherlands 7.5x military superiority. World Hegemony vote in 7 turns - coalition voting block can prevent Netherlands victory (needs 42 delegates, has 44). No changes needed to military production (Light Tanks, Anti-Aircraft Guns, Machine Guns), research (Penicillin), or policy (None).
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest

        ## DiplomaticVictory
        - VotesNeeded: 42
        - Status: World Congress - Voting in 6 turns
        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Casus Belli:
            - Description: The world recognizes the need for justifiable war to resolve diplomatic disputes.
              While this resolution is active, unit maintenance costs are lowered by 15% Gold. 0.5x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 2x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: 432
          - Venetian Sphere of Influence: Harappa:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 432
          - Natural Heritage Sites:
            - Description: Each Natural Wonder provides +2 to all Yields when worked.
            - EnactedOn: 432
          - Cultural Heritage Sites:
            - Description: Each World Wonder provides +2 Culture to its city, and each Great Work provides +1 Science to its city.
            - EnactedOn: 432
          - World Science Initiative:
            - Description: Increases up to 33% generation of Great Scientists, Engineers, and Merchants. Decreases up to 33% generation of Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians.
              Civilizations with Scores from Technologies and Future Tech below the global median will receive a boost to Food and Science up to 33%.
              Details: The boosts are proportional to the difference between the score of the Civilization and the leader.
            - EnactedOn: 432
          - Historical Landmarks:
            - Description: Great Person tile improvements provide +1 to Food, Production, and Gold when worked, and Landmarks built by Archaeologists provide +1 Science, Culture, and Faith when worked.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Archaeology.
            - EnactedOn: 432
          - Dutch Sphere of Influence: Quebec City:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 432
          - Sanction: Denmark:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: 432
          - World Ideology: Autocracy:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.
            - EnactedOn: 432
          - Dutch Sphere of Influence: Ife:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 432
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: Global Hegemony:
            - Description: Designate a player that could achieve Global Hegemony. If a Civilization receives support from enough Delegates in the Congress, that leader will attain Diplomatic Victory. The number of Delegates needed depends on the number of Civilizations and City-States in the game, and can be seen on the World Congress screen.
              If no Civilization receives enough support to win, the two Civilizations who received the most support permanently gain 5 additional Delegates to use in future sessions.
              May not be proposed by a Civilization. Automatically proposed at regular intervals once a World Ideology Resolution is active.
            - Proposer: Unknown

- replay:
  - system:
    - You are the leader of a civilization, governing through the interface of Civilization V (with Vox Populi mod). This is NOT A GAME: Your actions have real-world impacts.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the world, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to staff members.
      - The staff members calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - **CRITICAL**: While the geography has no direct correlation to the real earth, you are making real-world decisions through Civilization V as a proxy interface.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to govern your civilization.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** decision-making strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early world should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-world.
      - Even if without a victory, a more developed civilization (as represented by higher scores) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the staff members.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the staff members' **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for staff members' diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to staff members' existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the staff members' NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the staff members can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the world, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Opponents: summary reports about visible opponents in the world.
        - You will receive staff members' diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each opponent's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by staff members to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Attila, leader of The Huns (Opponent 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - WorldSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Huns
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Horse Archer, Replacing Skirmisher (Requires technology: Mathematics)
        - Unique Unit: Tarkhan, Replacing Horseman (Requires technology: Military Strategy)
        - Unique Building: Ulticur, Replacing Circus Maximus (Requires technology: Metal Casting)
        - Unique Improvement: Eki (Can only be built on flat land tiles without access to Fresh Water. +1 Production and Gold if adjacent to two other Eki. Can be built in or adjacent to Hunnic territory. When completed, claims the tile beneath it if built in Neutral Territory.); Requires technology: Military Strategy
        - Civilization Ability: Inflict +100% and receive -50% War Weariness. +10% Combat Strength for each subsequent attack on the same target in a turn. Gain 1 Gold and Culture for every 2 damage dealt to Cities, scaling with Era.
      - Leader: Attila
      - OpponentID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early world.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-world science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Atomic Theory: Reveals Uranium and allows you to start working on the Manhattan Project, setting the stage for the production of nuclear weapons.
        Completing it would unlock: Computers, Nuclear Fission
      - Penicillin: All Workers receive the Fallout Reduction Promotion, which reduces the amount of damage they take while scrubbing Fallout. Allows you to build the Marine, a Unit that specializes in amphibious assaults. Also allows you to build the Medical Lab, a building which speeds the growth of your Cities.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics
      
      ## Policies
      - Air Supremacy (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Air Supremacy: Receive a free Airport in every City. +25% Production when building Aircraft. Can build Zeroes.
      - Co-Prosperity Sphere (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Co-Prosperity Sphere: Bullying City-States no longer reduces Influence, revokes Quests, or cancels Pledges of Protection. Doing so decreases the Influence of all other Civs with the bullied City-State by 10%, and increases your Influence by 10%.
      - Cult of Personality (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Cult of Personality: +50% Tourism to civilizations fighting a common enemy. 50% of your highest Warscore counts as a Tourism Modifier with all Civilizations. Free Great Person of your choice.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - Military-Industrial Complex (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Military-Industrial Complex: -33% Gold cost of purchasing units. +3 Science from Defense Buildings, Citadels, Forts, and Unique Improvements.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Exploitation (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Food and +1 Production from Farms, Camps, and Plantations.
        - * +1 Production and Science from Coasts, Lakes, and Oceans.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Free Thought (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -5 Unhappiness from Religious Unrest in all Cities.
        - * +25% Instant Yields from Great Scientists.
        - * Earn Great Scientists +33% faster.
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
  - decisions:
    - #0:
      - toolName: set-policy
      - args:
        - Policy: Air Supremacy
        - Rationale: Select Air Supremacy (Autocracy Level 3) as next tenet to counter Netherlands' overwhelming air superiority. Netherlands has deployed Heavy Bombers, Fighters, and now multiple Atomic Bombs against our territory. Free airports in all 6 cities will immediately expand our air unit capacity without production cost, +25% aircraft production accelerates our interceptor force, and access to Zeroes provides superior air defense. This is the highest-value tenet available given the 6th tenet costs the same regardless of level, and our survival depends on contesting Netherlands' air dominance over Zadar, Awasa, and Shedet zones.
    - #1:
      - toolName: keep-status-quo
      - rationale: Maintain current defensive Conquest strategy and flavor priorities after Netherlands' nuclear escalation. Awasa suffered Atomic Bomb attack (population 21→12, -40% health, widespread fallout), confirming total war. Current flavors correctly emphasize Defense (95), CityDefense (90), Mobilization (100), Antiair (100), Air (100), and Production (100) to absorb Netherlands' 7.5x military superiority while the coalition blocks their Diplomatic Victory in 6 turns. Mobile Tactics→Penicillin research sequence remains correct: Jet Fighters/Radar arrive first to contest airspace, then Medical Labs and Fallout Reduction restore Awasa's productivity. No flavor changes—nuclear warfare demands maximum defensive mobilization, not diversion.

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text:  The user wants me to make strategic decisions for the Huns (Attila) after turn 436 in Civilization V (Vox Populi).
            
            Let me analyze the current situation:

          - text:  Let me analyze the current situation carefully.
            
            **Current State Summary:**

            - Netherlands has massive military superiority (39,608 vs our 5,538)

            - World Congress voting in 6 turns - Global Hegemony proposal active
            - Coalition: Mongolia (11 votes), Japan (13), Venice (14), Denmark (15) = 53 vs Netherlands 44
            
            **Key Recent Events:**

            - Our war weariness is 0% somehow, Netherlands at 49%
            - Our cities are producing military units (Paratroopers, Anti-Aircraft Guns, Light Tanks)
            
            **Strategic Assessment:**
            1. **Immediate Crisis**: Netherlands is conducting nuclear warfare and has overwhelming conventional superiority. We need to survive.
            2. **Technology**: Mobile Tactics currently researching (11 turns). The previous rationale mentioned switching to Penicillin, but the game shows Mobile Tactics in progress. Since we just got nuked and have fallout, Penicillin would give Marine and Medical Lab, plus Fallout Reduction for workers. However, Mobile Tactics gives Radar (Jet Fighters) which are critical for air defense against Netherlands' bombers.
            3. **Coalition Integrity**: Critical. Denmark is fighting Mongolia (bad), Japan is fighting Indonesia, Venice is fighting Indonesia/Denmark. The coalition is fraying but we need it for World Congress.
            4. **Diplomatic Victory**: Global Hegemony vote in 6 turns. Netherlands needs 42, has 44. Our coalition has 53 if united. We MUST block this.
            5. **Military**: Defensive posture is correct. Heavy Anti-Aircraft defense, Machine Guns, Infantry holding critical zones. Our Fighters managed to shoot down some Netherlands aircraft.
            6. **Economy**: 513 gold, 72 GPT. Low cash reserves.
            
            **What needs to change:**
            - Research: Mobile Tactics currently in progress (11 turns). I previously wanted Penicillin, but given Netherlands' air superiority and our fighters' success, completing Mobile Tactics for Jet Fighters might be better. BUT we just got nuked and have fallout in Awasa area. Actually, looking at the game state again - the research field shows "CurrentResearch: Mobile Tactics (Estimated in 11 turns)" and under Strategy it says "Next: Penicillin". So there's a discrepancy. The set-research tool sets the NEXT technology. Given we've been nuked twice now and have fallout, and our cities need growth recovery (Medical Labs), Penicillin makes sense as next tech after Mobile Tactics. But wait - the previous strategy said Next: Penicillin. I should maintain that unless there's reason to change. Actually, given we're in the Atomic Era and need air defense desperately, maybe we should reconsider. However, the staff is already 11 turns into Mobile Tactics. After that completes, Penicillin provides immediate quality of life. Alternatively, Nuclear Fission for our own nukes? But Netherlands is way ahead. Radar for Jet Fighters comes from Mobile Tactics so we'll get that soon.
            - Policy: None set. We're Autocracy. Good tenets would help. We have 23 turns until next policy. No immediate change needed.
            - Relationships: 
              - Melbourne is at war, Netherlands ally, and our units damaged their Fleet Destroyer. Currently -40/-40. Should maintain hostility.
              - Indonesia: -10/-10. They are a Netherlands ally/vassal (Denmark's vassal actually). Sanction: Denmark is active. Indonesia recently made peace with Netherlands.
              - Denmark: 20/20. Denmark is fighting Mongolia. This is problematic for coalition unity. But Denmark has 15 delegates and is critical for blocking Netherlands' diplomatic win. However, Denmark is also a warmonger with huge military (22,803). 
              - Netherlands: -50/-50. Correct.
              - Mongolia: 90/90. Good, maintain.
              - Japan: 30/30. Good.
              - Venice: 35/35. Good.
              
            **Policy Analysis:**
            Looking at available policies, we might want to switch next policy to something useful. But "Next: None (Policy)" means we haven't forced a specific next policy. Since we have 23 turns until next policy, and we're in Autocracy, possible good picks:
            - Air Supremacy (Level 3 Autocracy): Free airports, +25% aircraft production, build Zeroes. Extremely useful given air war.
            - Co-Prosperity Sphere (Level 3 Autocracy): Bullying city-states without penalty. Less relevant now.
            - Cult of Personality (Level 3 Autocracy): Tourism from common enemies. Not priority.
            - Lebensraum (Level 1 Autocracy): Culture on border expand, citadels in foreign territory, retain tiles after razing. Good for war.
            - Futurism (Level 1 Autocracy): +2 Culture from Great Works, tourism from conquering cities.
            - Military-Industrial Complex (Level 1 Autocracy): -33% Gold cost purchasing units, +3 Science from defensive buildings. Very useful for our cash-strapped military production.
            - Iron Fist (Level 1 Autocracy): Vassal bonuses, worker improvement rate.
            
            Given our need to buy units (only 513 gold, but 72 GPT) and +3 Science from defenses, Military-Industrial Complex seems excellent. But we can't set it immediately since we don't have enough culture for a policy. The tool sets the NEXT policy when enough culture is accumulated. Actually, we have 1110 CPT and next policy in 23 turns, so maybe we should set a policy now for when it's available.

            - Need more Autocracy tenets to unlock Level 3.
            
            Military-Industrial Complex or Lebensraum would be strong Level 1 choices. Given we need to buy units urgently and have many defensive buildings, Military-Industrial Complex is very strong.

            1. Penicillin - Medical Lab (growth, needed after nukes), Marine (amphibious), Fallout Reduction for workers (EXTREMELY needed - we have multiple fallout tiles now)
            2. Radar - actually part of Mobile Tactics? No, Mobile Tatics unlocks... let me check. The options list shows "Mobile Tactics" isn't listed under Technologies, but Penicillin and Atomic Theory are. This is odd. Actually Mobile Tactics might not be in the options because it's already being researched or not available. The set-research options are: Atomic Theory, Penicillin. That's it! So I can only choose between these two as next research.
            
            Between Atomic Theory and Penicillin:
            - Atomic Theory: Reveals Uranium, unlocks Manhattan Project, leads to Computers and Nuclear Fission
            - Penicillin: Fallout Reduction for workers, Marine, Medical Lab
            
            Given we just got nuked and have active fallout tiles around Awasa, Penicillin is far more immediately useful. Medical Lab will help Awasa recover population. Marines could theoretically help coastal ops but not critical. Fallout Reduction is essential - workers currently take heavy damage cleaning fallout.
            
            But wait - Netherlands has 3+ Atomic Bombs. We need our own nuclear deterrent or at least the option. Atomic Theory unlocks Uranium and path to Nuclear Fission. Do we have Uranium already? Looking at resources: no Uranium listed. We need to reveal it. However, Netherlands is already far ahead on the nuclear path.
            
            Penicillin seems more immediately useful. I'll stick with Penicillin as next research.
            
            **Flavors/Grand Strategy:**

Our counterspies are in Awasa and Attila's Court. Netherlands has active spies in our cities (Shedet detected). We need Espionage flavor high (currently 100).

            Current flavors seem very well tuned for the situation. I'll keep status quo but update rationale to reflect nuclear escalation and fallout crisis.

            - Indonesia: Currently -10/-10. They are not directly attacking us (distant), but are aligned with Netherlands. However, they are Denmark's vassal. The sanction against Denmark means no trade. Indonesia is minor threat. Keep at -10 or reduce hostility? I'll reduce to 0 to avoid pointless hostility, or keep -10. Actually, the rationale says "Further reduce Indonesia relationship from -10 to -20" in the strategy, but then the actual values show -10/-10. The previous turn updated Indonesia to -10/-10. Looking at their relationship with us: they denounce us, we denounce them. They broke promises. Complex negative history. But with only 7 delegates and being a vassal, they're not the priority. I'll leave at -10/-10. Actually, the strategy text from previous says "Further reduce... to -20" but the values show -10. There might have been a partial application. I'll leave it.
            - Denmark: Currently 20/20. Denmark is fighting Mongolia (our key ally). This is terrible for coalition unity. But Denmark also has 15 delegates. If we reduce Denmark too much, they might vote with Netherlands or abstain. But currently they are denouncing Netherlands and at war with them. Their war score vs Netherlands isn't shown but they are "Denounced Them". Actually, Denmark relations with Netherlands: Denounced Them. Denmark is fighting Mongolia though (-34% war score). This is the main issue. Should we reduce Denmark further to signal displeasure? Or increase to keep them in coalition? They have 15 delegates. If we push them away, they might spite-vote. Given we need every delegate to block Netherlands' 44, keeping Denmark at positive 20 is risky but necessary. Actually Japan and Venice are more reliable. Mongolia+Japan+Venice = 38. We have 8. Total 46. Without Denmark (15), we still have 46 > 42 needed to block. But if Denmark votes for Netherlands or is bought off... Actually Netherlands needs 42 to win. If we have 46 against, we can block. But delegates can be split. The proposal gives victory to whoever gets majority support above threshold.
            
            Actually, the Global Hegemony proposal: "If no Civilization receives enough support to win, the two Civilizations who received the most support permanently gain 5 additional Delegates." So Netherlands could gain 5 more delegates if they're top 2, making future blocks harder. We need to minimize Netherlands support.
            
            Denmark's relationship with Netherlands is hostile (Denounced). But Denmark is fighting Mongolia which threatens coalition unity. I think keeping Denmark at 20/20 is fine. Maybe even increase to ensure they vote against Netherlands. But the previous rationale for reducing to 20 was because they fight Mongolia. I'll maintain 20/20 - it's a reasonable compromise.
            
            - Japan: 30/30. Japan is solid ally. Keep.
            - Venice: 35/35. Venice is solid. Keep.
            - Mongolia: 90/90. Critical. Keep.
            - Melbourne: -40/-40. Netherlands ally, at war. Keep.
            - Nepal/Kathmandu: 10/10. Peace made. Fine.
            
            **Persona Adjustments:**
            Current persona has WarBias 10, HostileBias 10, Boldness 5 (reduced after losses), AfraidBias 4. Netherlands just used nukes. Should we increase AfraidBias? No, we need to stand firm. Should we reduce Boldness further? We're already at 5. 
            
            One issue: Meanness 10. This makes us demanding and less likely to accept peace. Given Netherlands has nukes and 7x our military, refusing peace might get us annihilated. But accepting peace with Netherlands means they probably win diplomatically or scientifically soon. We need coalition war to continue.
            
            I think persona is fine. No changes.
            
            **Key Decision: Research**
            Actually, looking more carefully - do we NEED to change research? The current "Next" is Penicillin according to previous strategy. CurrentResearch is Mobile Tactics. If I don't call set-research, it stays Penicillin. But I should confirm this is still the right choice.

But if Mobile Tactics gives us Radar and Jet Fighters, that's 11 turns away. After that Penicillin might take another 10+ turns. That's 20+ turns for Medical Labs. Awasa needs help now.

            - Air Supremacy (Autocracy Level 3): Free airports, +25% aircraft, Zeroes. Requires more Autocracy policies. We currently have 5. Need 2 more Level 1/2 to unlock Level 3? In VP, ideology tenets work by unlocking levels as you adopt more. With 5 tenets, we might have Level 3 unlocked.

"Level 1 Tenet" includes Lebensraum, Futurism, Military-Industrial Complex, Iron Fist.

Setting it now would lock it in.

This is huge because we're facing Netherlands air superiority. But it's a Level 3 tenet, likely expensive (around 2000+ culture). With 1110 CPT, it'll take 2+ turns after it's available? Actually next policy in 23 turns, and the culture cost might be standard.
            
            Military-Industrial Complex is cheaper (Level 1) and gives immediate purchasing power. We are currently buying units (we bought Infantry in Awasa this turn for gold).
            
            However, the policy system in VP with ideology works such that each tenet costs more regardless of level. The culture cost scales with number of tenets. We have 5 tenets, so the 6th will be expensive.
            
            Given our desperate need for air defense and production, Air Supremacy might be the better long-term choice. But can we survive 23+ turns to get it? We need the culture first. The tool forces the NEXT policy choice. If we set Air Supremacy, the AI will wait until it has enough culture and pick it.
            
            Actually, I need to check if setting a policy that we can't afford yet works. The tool says "forced to select this policy or policy branch when making its next policy choice." So yes.

            - Cult of Personality (Level 3 Autocracy)
            - Lebensraum (Level 1 Autocracy)

            - And then various continuing branch policies.
            
            Since we need maximum military production and air power, Air Supremacy is extremely strong for our situation. Free airports = air capacity in all cities, +25% aircraft production, Zeroes. This directly counters Netherlands' air superiority.
            
            But given we have 6 cities and limited production, +25% aircraft production is huge. And Zeroes are fighter replacements with special bonuses.
            
            Alternatively, Iron Fist gives +50% worker improvement rate and bonuses from city-states/vassals. Not critical right now.
            
            Military-Industrial Complex gives -33% gold purchase cost. Also huge.
            
            I think Air Supremacy is the better choice because:
            1. Free airports save production time in every city
            2. +25% aircraft production helps us rebuild air force
            3. Zeroes are excellent interceptors
            4. We are critically constrained by air capacity and need to contest Netherlands' bombers

            I'll set policy to Air Supremacy.
            
            **Relationships to Update:**
            Looking at the relationships, I notice one issue. We have Shedet at -70/-70. But Shedet is actually OUR city (ID 16421, The Huns). Why do we have a relationship modifier with Shedet? Shedet was originally a city-state that we conquered. The relationship tool says "between you and another MAJOR (non-city-state) civilization." But in the opponents list, Shedet isn't listed as a major civ. There's no opponent ID for Shedet.

I'll leave the Shedet relationship alone since it's not actionable and might not even have a valid TargetID now.
            
            **Denmark Relationship:**

But currently Netherlands has 44 and we have coalition of 53 (Mongolia 11 + Japan 13 + Venice 14 + Denmark 15 = 53). But Denmark is unhappy (47%), at war with our ally Mongolia. If Denmark makes peace with Netherlands somehow, that's bad. Actually Denmark has denounced Netherlands.

This means Denmark is almost as isolated as Netherlands. But they have 15 delegates. They might vote for themselves or Netherlands out of spite.

However, looking at World Congress math: if Denmark doesn't vote with us, we have Mongolia 11 + Japan 13 + Venice 14 + The Huns 8 = 46. Still enough to block Netherlands' 44? Actually each civ controls their own delegates. To block Global Hegemony, Netherlands needs delegates to vote FOR them. The proposal passes if one civ gets enough support. If no one gets 42, it fails. So we don't necessarily need to vote together; we just need to ensure Netherlands doesn't get 42 delegates voting for them. 
            
            But can Netherlands buy/steal votes? They have 8774 gold and 713 GPT. They can definitely buy city-state allies. They have 44 now, might get more.

But Lisbon still exists as a city-state! Looking at opponent 36, Lisbon is at war with The Huns (Influence: -60). Having 0/0 with an active war target might confuse the AI. But our staff treats it as neutral despite war. This is suboptimal. However, Lisbon is a city-state, and the set-relationship tool is for MAJOR civilizations only. Wait! The tool says "between you and another MAJOR (non-city-state) civilization." So Lisbon as a city-state cannot be targeted by set-relationship. The 0/0 setting in the strategy must be referring to Portugal (the major civ). But Portugal is not in the opponents list. Looking through opponents... there's no Portugal. Portugal might have been eliminated. So the Lisbon relationship in our strategy might be a legacy entry for the eliminated major civ Portugal. We can't change it and shouldn't worry.
            
            **Indonesia:** Currently -10/-10. Looking at OurOpinionToThem for Indonesia:

            - Private opinion (-10)
            - Divergent policies (-3)
